# Swarm Operation Tracker

This file tracks multi-agent Codex work for ZMem so swarm state is visible outside transient chat notifications.

## Current Operation

Mission: make ZMem the best memory product for AI agents: frontier-grade on benchmarks and real-world recall quality, while preserving the proof-backed memory influence wedge.

North star:

- Win trust through local-first privacy, portability, governance, receipts, Merkle proof, handoff, and verification.
- Win product quality through excellent retrieval, temporal reasoning, update/conflict handling, abstention, latency, token efficiency, and multi-hop recall.
- Use the proof system as the credibility layer for benchmark claims, not as an excuse to be weaker on memory quality.

Coordinator thread: current Codex thread titled `Zerker Memory`.

Primary mission brief:

- `docs/BENCHMARK_RETRIEVAL_SWARM_MISSION.md`
- Continuous frontier-gap orchestrator: `docs/ZMEM_CONTINUOUS_BUILD_ORCHESTRATOR.md`
- Lane logs: `docs/CONTINUOUS_BUILD/`

### Swarm A: Benchmark Harness

Goal: build `zmem bench` into a local-first benchmark harness with receipts, hashes, Merkle roots, reports, and tamper verification so ZMem can honestly climb toward top-tier benchmark performance.

Completed agents:

- `Poincare` - benchmark harness explorer. Produced the implementation map for `zmem bench synthetic`.
- `Darwin` - benchmark proof worker. Implemented the first local proof-bearing benchmark slice.

- `2026-06-11` - Benchmark HTML proof hops surfaced. The generated matrix dashboard/public benchmark page now render a browser-visible `Per-Mode Proof Hops` section from the existing `mode_proofs` contract, so each retrieval mode's `result_hash` and `aggregate_merkle_root` is visible in HTML without opening raw result JSON.
- Why this slice mattered: the previous slice made the per-mode proof hop durable in matrix JSON, Markdown, and summary-only CLI output, but browser-first review still hid the final proof hop behind raw artifact inspection.

Latest coordinator retrieval slice:

- `2026-06-09` - Phrase/exact boosted baseline ranking. Retrieval now turns receipt-visible phrase/exact-match features into actual strong-FTS candidate ordering, so higher-trust lexical decoys no longer automatically outrank tighter canonical facts once the query planner has already found the right phrase family.
- Why this slice mattered: the prior canonical-relation query planning preserved the decisive terms for terse facts, but the default baseline still left final ordering mostly to SQLite authority/trust sorting, which meant the best phrase match could still land behind a broader higher-trust decoy until later overlays or packing pressure exposed the miss.
- Verification: `python3 -m unittest tests.test_store -q` passed (`73` tests), `python3 -m unittest tests.test_policy -q` passed (`7` tests), `python3 -m unittest tests.test_runner -q` passed (`16` tests), and `python3 -m zerker_memory eval` passed (`11/11`); `rg -n "zerkerlabs/zerker-memory|raw\.githubusercontent\.com/zerkerlabs/zerker-memory|github\.com/zerkerlabs/zerker-memory" README.md QUICKSTART.md install.sh scripts tests zerker_memory docs landing llms.txt site --glob '!docs/BUILD_LOG.md' --glob '!docs/CURRENT_STATE.md' --glob '!docs/SWARM_OPERATION_TRACKER.md' --glob '!docs/PRODUCT_STATUS.md' --glob '!docs/PUBLIC_LAUNCH_AUDIT.md'` returned no active-surface stale-target matches.
- Current blocker: same-phrase facts can still tie when neither a full exact query match nor a unique contiguous phrase separates them, because the deterministic baseline still lacks stronger field-aware boosts across labels, memory type, and source role.
- Next retrieval slice: add deterministic field-aware label/type/source-kind boosts for same-phrase ties while keeping `rank_before_boosts`, score components, and budget/injection receipts explainable.

- `2026-06-09` - Temporal-selection-aware context packing. Retrieval now preserves history/timeline intent through the packing stage by using temporal selection rank when a query already selected an older-then-newer sequence, instead of letting a fresher current fact retake priority under a tight context budget.
- Why this slice mattered: chronology selection was already correct in `retrieval["temporal"]["selected_ids"]`, but a budgeted injection could still discard the earliest timeline fact and keep only the newer current fact because the packer fell back to raw retrieval rank plus a `current` bonus after temporal resolution had already done the right thing.
- Verification: `python3 -m unittest tests.test_store -q` passed (`71` tests), `python3 -m unittest tests.test_policy -q` passed (`7` tests), `python3 -m unittest tests.test_runner -q` passed (`16` tests), and `python3 -m zerker_memory eval` passed (`11/11`); `rg -n "zerkerlabs/zerker-memory|raw\.githubusercontent\.com/zerkerlabs/zerker-memory|github\.com/zerkerlabs/zerker-memory" README.md QUICKSTART.md install.sh scripts tests zerker_memory docs landing llms.txt site --glob '!docs/BUILD_LOG.md' --glob '!docs/CURRENT_STATE.md' --glob '!docs/SWARM_OPERATION_TRACKER.md' --glob '!docs/PRODUCT_STATUS.md' --glob '!docs/PUBLIC_LAUNCH_AUDIT.md'` returned no active-surface stale-target matches.
- Current blocker: temporal ordering now survives packing budgets, but chronology prompts still depend too much on generic lexical overlap to retrieve the right candidate set before temporal selection begins.
- Next retrieval slice: add query-side chronology expansion for `when` / `after` / `before` / `first` / `then` prompts while keeping candidate reasoning, packing, stale/current, and withheld metadata explainable.

- `2026-06-09` - Subject-led canonical `uses` / `requires` short-object preservation. Retrieval now recognizes direct canonical subject-led facts across `uses` and `requires` as deterministic relation query plans, so the first planned FTS variant preserves short target terms such as `db` and `ui` instead of dropping them through the generic `query_terms()` path.
- Why this slice mattered: the earlier canonical subject-led preservation already covered `points to`, `belongs to`, `deploys to`, and `runs on`, but terse direct prompts like `deploy service uses db` or `deploy job requires ui` still over-pruned the decisive object token and relied too much on weaker raw-query fallback.
- Verification: `python3 -m unittest tests.test_store -q` passed (`70` tests), `python3 -m unittest tests.test_policy -q` passed (`7` tests), `python3 -m unittest tests.test_runner -q` passed (`16` tests), and `python3 -m zerker_memory eval` passed (`11/11`); `rg -n "zerkerlabs/zerker-memory|raw\.githubusercontent\.com/zerkerlabs/zerker-memory|github\.com/zerkerlabs/zerker-memory" README.md QUICKSTART.md install.sh scripts tests zerker_memory docs landing llms.txt site --glob '!docs/BUILD_LOG.md' --glob '!docs/CURRENT_STATE.md' --glob '!docs/SWARM_OPERATION_TRACKER.md' --glob '!docs/PRODUCT_STATUS.md' --glob '!docs/PUBLIC_LAUNCH_AUDIT.md'` returned no active-surface stale-target matches.
- Current blocker: direct canonical relation query coverage is now complete for the current strong-FTS family, but terse overlapping facts can still benefit from stronger phrase/exact-match ranking so the top candidate separates the true relation fact from nearby lexical decoys earlier.
- Next retrieval slice: add deterministic phrase/exact-match boosts for the strong FTS baseline while keeping candidate reasoning, packing, and withheld metadata explainable.

- `2026-06-08` - Subject-led canonical relation short-token preservation. Retrieval now recognizes direct canonical subject-led facts across `points to`, `belongs to`, `deploys to`, and `runs on` as deterministic relation query plans, so the first planned FTS variant preserves connector tokens plus short target terms such as `to`, `on`, `v2`, `ui`, `qa`, and `db` instead of dropping them through the generic `query_terms()` path.
- Why this slice mattered: the object-led and question-led relation planners were already preserving meaningful short tokens, but direct canonical prompts like `api gateway points to v2` or `deploy service runs on db` still over-pruned the decisive relation/object terms and leaned too heavily on weaker raw-query fallback.
- Verification: `python3 -m unittest tests.test_store -q` passed (`68` tests), `python3 -m unittest tests.test_policy -q` passed (`7` tests), `python3 -m unittest tests.test_runner -q` passed (`14` tests), and `python3 -m zerker_memory eval` passed (`11/11`); `rg -n "zerkerlabs/zerker-memory|raw\.githubusercontent\.com/zerkerlabs/zerker-memory|github\.com/zerkerlabs/zerker-memory" README.md QUICKSTART.md install.sh scripts tests zerker_memory docs landing llms.txt site --glob '!docs/BUILD_LOG.md' --glob '!docs/CURRENT_STATE.md' --glob '!docs/SWARM_OPERATION_TRACKER.md' --glob '!docs/PRODUCT_STATUS.md' --glob '!docs/PUBLIC_LAUNCH_AUDIT.md'` returned no active-surface stale-target matches.
- Current blocker: direct canonical short-object lookups for `uses` and `requires` still flow through the generic subject path, so terse prompts like `service uses db` or `job requires ui` can still drop the decisive two-character target token in their first planned FTS query.
- Next retrieval slice: extend the same receipt-visible direct canonical short-token preservation to subject-led `uses` / `requires` query plans while keeping `MemoryStore.search()` output stable.

- `2026-06-08` - Utility-aware context packing. Budgeted retrieval packing now uses a deterministic priority-knapsack selector across already-authorized memories, so fixed-budget injections can retain a higher-total-value subset of compact relevant memories instead of always truncating at the first verbose top-ranked match, while `retrieval["packing"]` remains receipt-visible with per-candidate priorities and budget-drop metadata.
- Why this slice mattered: the previous packer was still spending scarce context budget in pure rank order, which meant one long high-ranked memory could crowd out multiple smaller complementary memories even after retrieval, temporal selection, and policy authorization had already done the right filtering.
- Verification: `python3 -m unittest tests.test_store -q` passed (`64` tests), `python3 -m unittest tests.test_policy -q` passed (`7` tests), `python3 -m unittest tests.test_runner -q` passed (`13` tests), and `python3 -m zerker_memory eval` passed (`11/11`).
- Current blocker: subject-led canonical relation variants still strip connector and short target tokens in their first planned FTS query, so short-target prompts like `deploy to qa`, `run on db`, or `point to v2` still lean too heavily on raw-query fallback.
- Next retrieval slice: extend the same relation-aware short-token preservation to subject-led `deploys_to` / `runs_on` / `points_to` / `belongs_to` query plans while preserving receipt explainability and stable `MemoryStore.search()` output.

- `2026-06-08` - Object-led relation query planning. Retrieval now rewrites relation-first object-led prompts such as `What runs on Kubernetes?`, `What is deployed to production?`, and the same family across `uses`, `points to`, `belongs to`, and `requires` into deterministic relation-specific search variants, while keeping the matched alias family receipt-visible through `query_lookup.lookup_basis`.
- Why this slice mattered: the prior passive/prepositional subject-led normalization still let object-led prompts collapse into broad question-wrapper searches like `runs kubernetes` or `deployed production`, which pulled decoys that only overlapped on the verb plus object words instead of the actual relation.
- Verification: `python3 -m unittest tests.test_store -q` passed (`63` tests), `python3 -m unittest tests.test_policy -q` passed (`7` tests), `python3 -m unittest tests.test_runner -q` passed (`13` tests), and `python3 -m zerker_memory eval` passed (`11/11`); `rg -n "zerkerlabs/zerker-memory|raw\.githubusercontent\.com/zerkerlabs/zerker-memory|github\.com/zerkerlabs/zerker-memory" README.md QUICKSTART.md install.sh scripts tests zerker_memory docs landing llms.txt site --glob '!docs/BUILD_LOG.md' --glob '!docs/CURRENT_STATE.md' --glob '!docs/SWARM_OPERATION_TRACKER.md' --glob '!docs/PRODUCT_STATUS.md' --glob '!docs/PUBLIC_LAUNCH_AUDIT.md'` returned no active-surface stale-target matches.
- Current blocker: object-led relation-first prompts are now covered, but subject-led canonical relation variants still drop connector and short target tokens in their first planned FTS terms, so short-target queries such as `deploy to qa`, `run on db`, or `point to v2` still depend too much on raw-query fallback.
- Next retrieval slice: extend the same relation-aware short-token preservation to subject-led `deploys_to` / `runs_on` / `points_to` / `belongs_to` query plans while keeping `query_lookup` explainability and stable `search()` output.

- `2026-06-08` - Passive/prepositional relation-family normalization. Retrieval now rewrites the remaining subject-led passive/prepositional prompts such as `What is required by ...?`, `What is ... part of?`, `Where is ... deployed?`, and `What is ... running on?` into the same canonical subject-first search phrases used by stored facts, while keeping the matched alias family receipt-visible through `query_lookup.lookup_basis`.
- Why this slice mattered: the prior inverse/prepositional normalization closed `used by` and `point at`, but real agent phrasing across `requires`, `belongs to`, `deploys to`, and `runs on` still depended too heavily on the canonical stored wording, which kept current-state recall uneven on otherwise simple relation lookups.
- Verification: `python3 -m unittest tests.test_store -q` passed (`61` tests), `python3 -m unittest tests.test_policy -q` passed (`7` tests), `python3 -m unittest tests.test_runner -q` passed (`12` tests), and `python3 -m zerker_memory eval` passed (`11/11`); `rg -n "zerkerlabs/zerker-memory|raw\.githubusercontent\.com/zerkerlabs/zerker-memory|github\.com/zerkerlabs/zerker-memory" README.md QUICKSTART.md install.sh scripts tests zerker_memory docs landing llms.txt site --glob '!docs/BUILD_LOG.md' --glob '!docs/CURRENT_STATE.md' --glob '!docs/SWARM_OPERATION_TRACKER.md' --glob '!docs/PRODUCT_STATUS.md' --glob '!docs/PUBLIC_LAUNCH_AUDIT.md'` returned no active-surface stale-target matches.
- Current blocker: subject-led passive phrasing is now covered, but true object-led inverse queries for these relation families still rely on lexical overlap with the stored subject-first fact, so prompts like `What runs on Kubernetes?` or `What deploys to production?` remain underpowered.
- Next retrieval slice: add deterministic object-led inverse lookup planning for the same relation families while keeping `query_lookup` explainability and preserving abstention on genuine cross-source conflicts.

- `2026-06-07` - Inverse/prepositional relation-question normalization. Retrieval now rewrites inverse/object-led relation prompts such as `What is used by ...?` and broader prepositional phrasing such as `What does ... point at?` into the same canonical subject-first search phrases used by stored facts, while keeping the matched alias family receipt-visible through `query_lookup.lookup_basis`.
- Why this slice mattered: the prior relation-question normalization closed the highest-friction owner/uses questions, but real agent phrasing still missed on passive/object-led and `point at`-style wording because retrieval depended on stronger lexical overlap with the stored canonical fact string.
- Verification: `python3 -m unittest tests.test_store -q` passed (`57` tests), `python3 -m unittest tests.test_policy -q` passed (`7` tests), `python3 -m unittest tests.test_runner -q` passed (`11` tests), and `python3 -m zerker_memory eval` passed (`11/11`); `rg -n "zerkerlabs/zerker-memory|raw\.githubusercontent\.com/zerkerlabs/zerker-memory|github\.com/zerkerlabs/zerker-memory" README.md QUICKSTART.md install.sh scripts tests zerker_memory docs landing llms.txt site --glob '!docs/BUILD_LOG.md' --glob '!docs/CURRENT_STATE.md' --glob '!docs/SWARM_OPERATION_TRACKER.md' --glob '!docs/PRODUCT_STATUS.md' --glob '!docs/PUBLIC_LAUNCH_AUDIT.md'` returned no active-surface stale-target matches.
- Current blocker: passive/prepositional aliases are still only expanded for the highest-leverage `uses` and `points_to` families, so relation families such as `required by`, `runs on`, `deploys to`, and `belongs to` remain uneven unless the query is already close to the stored fact wording.
- Next retrieval slice: extend the same deterministic alias planner to the remaining passive/prepositional relation families while keeping `query_lookup` explainability and preserving abstention on genuine cross-source conflicts.

- `2026-06-07` - Relation-question retrieval normalization. Retrieval now rewrites current-state role/relation questions like `Who owns ...?` and `Which env does ... use?` into deterministic canonical search phrases before FTS/fallback lookup, while the temporal receipt path consumes the same query-lookup metadata so same-provenance restatements resolve and unresolved cross-provenance ties still abstain.
- Why this slice mattered: the previous broader `Who is ...?` / `What is ...?` fix still left real agent phrasing underpowered because FTS was matching raw wrapper verbs like `owns` and `does` instead of the stored canonical fact forms such as `X owner is Y` or `X uses Y`.
- Verification: `python3 -m unittest tests.test_store -q` passed (`55` tests), `python3 -m unittest tests.test_policy -q` passed (`7` tests), `python3 -m unittest tests.test_runner -q` passed (`9` tests), and `python3 -m zerker_memory eval` passed (`11/11`); `rg -n "zerkerlabs/zerker-memory|raw\.githubusercontent\.com/zerkerlabs/zerker-memory|github\.com/zerkerlabs/zerker-memory" README.md QUICKSTART.md install.sh scripts tests zerker_memory docs landing llms.txt site --glob '!docs/BUILD_LOG.md' --glob '!docs/CURRENT_STATE.md' --glob '!docs/SWARM_OPERATION_TRACKER.md' --glob '!docs/PRODUCT_STATUS.md' --glob '!docs/PUBLIC_LAUNCH_AUDIT.md'` returned no active-surface stale-target matches.
- Current blocker: inverse/object-led and broader prepositional relation paraphrases still depend on subject-first stored phrasing, so lookups like `What is used by X?`, `What does X point at?`, or other non-canonical relation wording remain weaker than the newly normalized owner/uses/points-to family.
- Next retrieval slice: extend deterministic query-side relation normalization to inverse/prepositional current-state lookups, while keeping receipt-visible query planning and preserving abstention on genuine cross-source conflicts.

- `2026-06-07` - Broad question-wrapper current-state resolution. Retrieval now strips question-wrapper terms plus article noise from same-provenance subject lookups, so broader `Who is ...?` / `What is ...?` current-state questions use the same observation-order restatement heuristic as terse lookups while still leaving genuine cross-provenance ties unresolved.
- Why this slice mattered: the prior subject-lookup fix covered terse and explicitly current-framed lookups, but natural-language agent questions were still underpowered and the richer local LongMemEval-style ambiguous-restatement `knowledge_update` case was still phrased as a full question.
- Verification: `python3 -m unittest tests.test_store -q` passed (`52` tests), `python3 -m unittest tests.test_policy -q` passed (`7` tests), `python3 -m unittest tests.test_runner -q` passed (`8` tests), `python3 -m unittest tests.test_bench -q` passed (`57` tests), and `python3 -m zerker_memory eval` passed (`11/11`); the richer local LongMemEval-style benchmark coverage now reflects the closed `knowledge_update` gap instead of the prior `2/3` miss.
- Current blocker: query-side role/relation paraphrases still depend on direct subject-string alignment, so lookups like `Who owns ...?` or `Which env does ... use?` remain weaker than `X owner is Y` / `X uses Y` stored forms unless stronger lexical overlap exists.
- Next retrieval slice: add deterministic query-side role/relation normalization for current-state lookups so paraphrased owner/uses/points-to questions improve without weakening abstention on genuine cross-source conflicts.

- `2026-06-07` - Subject-lookup restatement resolution. Retrieval now treats later observation order as explicit evidence for same-provenance plain restatements when the query is a terse subject lookup or an explicitly current-framed lookup such as `... now`/`current`/`latest`, while keeping the fuller unresolved current-conflict path intact.
- Why this slice mattered: the remaining honest retrieval gap had moved from explicit updates to plain restatements, especially benchmark-style `knowledge_update` lookups where the latest fact was phrased as another `X is Y` statement instead of `changed to` or `is now`.
- Verification: `python3 -m unittest tests.test_store -q` passed (`51` tests), `python3 -m unittest tests.test_policy -q` passed (`7` tests), `python3 -m unittest tests.test_runner -q` passed (`7` tests), `python3 -m unittest tests.test_bench -q` passed (`57` tests), and `python3 -m zerker_memory eval` passed (`11/11`).
- Current blocker: ambiguous same-subject restatements phrased as broader WH questions without explicit current cues still abstain unless there is stronger lifecycle/provenance evidence, which is safer than guessing but still leaves some natural-language current-state lookups underpowered.
- Next retrieval slice: extend current-state question matching beyond terse/current-framed lookups using stronger provenance-aware evidence so `Who is ...?` style questions improve without collapsing abstention on genuine cross-source conflicts.

Latest coordinator UX slice:

- `2026-06-06` - Console UX proof and benchmark panel. The console now exposes memory health, duplicate clusters, latest benchmark matrix evidence, and Treeship-style proven/asserted proof views.
- Why this slice mattered: dogfooding showed smoke-memory duplicates and raw receipt JSON were muddying the product story, while the attached Treeship boundary-proof note provided the right user-facing distinction for proof credibility.
- Next UX slice should make clusters actionable and replace raw proof reading with a receipt detail drawer.

Latest coordinator product-management slice:

- `2026-06-06` - Multi-workspace product spec. ZMem must become profile-aware so users can manage multiple Codex projects, Claude Code projects, manual MCP agents, long-running sessions, global/private memory, project memory, and imported handoff memory without cross-project leakage.
- Next implementation slice: `zmem workspace register/list/current/use/status`, automatic current-project registration, profile metadata in status, and a console workspace banner/mismatch warning.

### Swarm B: Retrieval Baseline

Goal: upgrade retrieval quality until ZMem is genuinely frontier-grade, while keeping search/injection auditable and reproducible.

Completed agents:

- `Turing` - retrieval baseline explorer. Mapped current search/inject path and first implementation slice.
- `Mendel` - retrieval plan worker. Wrote the concrete retrieval implementation plan.

Shipped files:

- `docs/RETRIEVAL_BASELINE_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`
- `docs/BENCHMARK_AND_RETRIEVAL_PLAN.md` link to the retrieval plan
- `zerker_memory/store.py`
- `tests/test_store.py`
- `tests/test_runner.py`

Latest shipped retrieval behavior:

- `search()` remains stable and still returns only memory rows.
- `search_with_meta()` now includes versioned retrieval metadata with ranked candidates, BM25 where available, deterministic score components, matched fields, mode, and limit.
- Search ordering now uses explicit authority weights: `none < low < medium < high < policy`.
- Fallback search records candidate metadata with `bm25: null`.
- `inject()` persists richer retrieval metadata in `retrieval_json`, so `why()` exposes benchmark-auditable ranking details.
- `MemoryStore.inject(..., context_budget_tokens=...)` can now apply deterministic context packing after policy authorization and before receipt construction.
- Budget-dropped authorized memories are omitted from injected context and recorded under `retrieval["packing"]["budget_dropped"]` with reason `context-budget`; `why()` preserves this metadata.
- Default/no-budget injection remains compatible with earlier behavior.
- `retrieval["temporal"]` now records lifecycle-based current/stale/superseded/expired decisions using existing parent links, timestamps, `expires_at`, status, and revocation behavior.
- Candidate features now include `temporal_state`, `superseded_by_candidate`, `child_candidate_ids`, `is_expired`, `updated_at`, and `expires_at`.
- If matching active parent and child memories are both retrieved, the child remains current/injectable and the parent is receipt-visible as superseded/stale.
- Expired active candidates remain receipt-visible but are not authorized, packed, or injected.
- `retrieval["temporal"]["conflict_sets"]` now records conservative audit metadata for explicit lifecycle evidence: active child superseding retrieved parent, expired retrieved candidates, and revoked candidates if they reach the resolver through a supported path.
- Unrelated matching candidates do not create conflict metadata, and `why()` preserves the sparse conflict sets.
- Query-aware temporal selection now lets explicit history queries such as `previous`, `before`, `earlier`, `prior`, and `original` inject superseded memories ahead of current memories while leaving default/current queries on the current-only path.
- `retrieval["temporal"]` now records `selection_strategy`, `selection_reason`, `selection_matched_terms`, and `selected_ids`, and candidates record `selected_by_temporal_strategy` plus `temporal_selection_rank` for receipt-visible explainability.
- `inject()` now authorizes the temporal resolver's selected memories instead of blindly restricting to `current_memories`, while `search()` and the base `MemoryStore.search()` return shape remain stable.
- Query-aware temporal selection now also treats chronology/timeline terms such as `when`, `after`, `first`, `then`, `timeline`, and `sequence` as an explicit chronological mode, selecting current plus superseded candidates in ascending created/updated order.
- `retrieval["temporal"]` now records `selection_order`, with chronology queries marked as `chronological_asc` and rank-based paths marked explicitly, so receipt-visible packing/injection order is explainable for timeline questions.
- Default/current queries now also resolve same-subject current-memory conflicts without schema changes: lexical fact groups such as `status page owner is ...` choose a single current winner by authority, trust, freshness, and prior rank instead of injecting contradictory current memories together.
- `retrieval["temporal"]["conflict_sets"]` now records `lexical-current-conflict` entries with `chosen_current_id`, `dropped_current_ids`, `subject_key`, `relation`, normalized value traces, and the deterministic `authority_trust_freshness_rank_v1` resolution strategy.
- Losing current-conflict candidates remain retrieved and receipt-visible but are excluded from `selected_ids`, `injected_memory_ids`, and runtime context unless an explicit history/chronology query asks for a broader temporal view.
- `retrieval["embedding"]` and `retrieval["reranker"]` now record local-first proof metadata by default without changing order: enabled state, model/reranker ids, config hashes, vector ids/hashes, rank transitions, score components, fallbacks, and disabled reasons.
- Opt-in internal `retrieval_config` can enable deterministic pseudo-embedding reorder and deterministic reranker plumbing. Receipts store ids/hashes and ranks only, never raw vectors.
- `retrieval["multi_hop"]` now records local deterministic decomposition metadata by default, disabled unless opted in.
- Opt-in `retrieval_config["multi_hop"]["enabled"]` unions parent-query and subquery candidates before embedding/reranking/temporal resolution, with receipt-visible subqueries, merge metadata, duplicate attribution, rank transitions, and candidate fields.
- Benchmark mode `fts-multihop` now enables multi-hop with embeddings/reranker off, records decomposition proof fields, and includes multi-hop rank fields in candidate-rank hashes.
- Explicit opt-in provider embedding is now available through provider config plus `allow_network_providers`, with local defaults unchanged, raw vectors/secrets excluded from receipts, provider-observed reproducibility labels, and a non-active candidate safety gate.

## Phase 1 Guardrail

Do not let benchmark/retrieval swarms touch:

- `.zerker/launch-proof/`
- `install.sh`
- `scripts/release_smoke.py`
- release-pack behavior
- prelaunch behavior
- public repo proof docs

Exception: run release/status smoke commands for verification only.

Current Phase 1 blockers remain:

- public verify logs: `0/6`
- launch assets: `0/8`
- strict publish: `no`

## Automation Model

Use two recurring/parallel tracks:

### Track 1: Benchmark Harness Swarm

Cadence: one bounded implementation slice per run.

Run prompt should point to:

- `docs/SWARM_OPERATION_TRACKER.md`
- `docs/BENCHMARK_RETRIEVAL_SWARM_MISSION.md`
- `docs/BENCHMARK_AGENT_SWARM_BRIEF.md`
- `docs/BENCHMARK_AND_RETRIEVAL_PLAN.md`

Output requirements:

- changed files
- verification commands and exact results
- benchmark command demo if behavior changed
- update this tracker

### Track 2: Retrieval Baseline Swarm

Cadence: one retrieval slice per run.

Run prompt should point to:

- `docs/SWARM_OPERATION_TRACKER.md`
- `docs/BENCHMARK_RETRIEVAL_SWARM_MISSION.md`
- `docs/RETRIEVAL_BASELINE_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`
- `docs/BENCHMARK_AND_RETRIEVAL_PLAN.md`

Output requirements:

- changed files
- retrieval behavior summary
- focused tests
- `python3 -m zerker_memory eval`
- update this tracker

## Tracking Rules

Every swarm run must update:

- `docs/SWARM_OPERATION_TRACKER.md`
- `docs/BUILD_LOG.md`
- `docs/CURRENT_STATE.md` if the project state or next recommended slice changes

For benchmark/retrieval behavior changes, run:

```bash
python3 -m unittest tests.test_bench -q
python3 -m unittest tests.test_store -q
python3 -m zerker_memory eval
```

If CLI or release-sensitive paths change, also run:

```bash
python3 scripts/release_smoke.py --summary-only
python3 -m zerker_memory status --summary-only
```
